It feels like those thoughts only stuck around while I was in that high-energy state.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, about how long did the episodes usually last, and did they ever clearly last at least one full week?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods have definitely lasted much longer than just a week. In fact, I've been experiencing these episodes on and off, and sometimes continuously, for about two years and two months now."

However, plain Bipolar I Disorder also remains possible because the belief appears tied to high-energy episodes and may represent severe grandiosity rather than definite psychosis; the patient is also uncertain about exact timing after mood normalization. Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Inflated Self-Confidence: Feeling capable of anything and believing that failure is impossible, unlike one's usual self.\n  - Overestimation of Abilities: Unrealistically assessing one's talents, knowledge, or abilities as superior.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one is a special and important person with a special mission or purpose.\n  - Talkativeness: Speaking far more than usual and continuing to talk without rest.\n  - Rapid Speech: Speech speed is so fast that it is difficult for others to understand or interrupt.\n  - Pressured Speech: Cannot stop talking or tolerate silence due to an internal pressure to keep speaking.\n  - Unrestrained Buying Sprees: Spending excessive amounts of money or shopping recklessly to a degree that creates financial distress for self and family.\n  - Foolish Business Investments: Making rash business investments or ventures without sufficient review.\n  - Sexual Indiscretions: Engaging in indiscriminate or risky sexual behavior that is out of character.\n  - Impulsive Risky Behavior: Excessively dangerous or foolish behavior, such as reckless driving or drug use, without considering the consequences.\n  Duration: 2 years and 2 months\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Grandiose Delusions\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that you have had strong beliefs about having a special, important mission or purpose. Explain that to others, this belief made you seem completely out of touch with reality, even though it felt absolutely real and crucial to you at the time."

    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I've been dealing with really extreme mood swings for about two years and two months now. Some days I feel incredibly energetic and happy, but then I'll suddenly get extremely irritable and angry over the smallest things. It's like I'm constantly shifting between these two intense states.\n\nDoctor: During your high-energy periods, have you ever gone at least a week needing much less sleep and feeling so energized or impulsive that it caused major problems, like being hospitalized, getting into serious trouble, or losing touch with reality?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about needing less sleep, but my energy definitely overflows to the point where I can't stay still. During those times, I've done some really reckless things without thinking of the consequences, and I've even believed I had a special, grand mission in this world.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs like having a special mission, or heard or seen things others didn’t, during a time when your mood was otherwise normal for at least 2 weeks? Or did those experiences only happen during your high-energy mood episodes?\n\nPatient: Those beliefs about having a special mission only happen during my high-energy phases. When my mood is completely normal, I don't experience anything like that. It's only been happening during those intense episodes over the last two years and two months.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy episodes, besides feeling you had a special mission, did you ever hear voices, see things others couldn’t, or become so convinced of unusual beliefs that others said you were out of touch with reality?\n\nPatient: No, I have never heard voices or seen things that weren't actually there. I just strongly believed that I was a special person with a unique mission to fulfill. Other than that, I didn't have any other unusual beliefs or experiences like that.\n\nDoctor: When you believed you had a special mission, were you so certain of it that other people thought it was clearly unrealistic or bizarre, or did it feel more like an unusually strong sense of purpose?\n\nPatient: I was absolutely convinced that I had a highly unique, special mission to save the world, and it was much more than just feeling motivated. My family and friends thought I had completely lost my mind and told me my ideas were totally unrealistic and impossible. To me, it felt like an absolute, undeniable truth at the time.\n\nDoctor: When you were convinced you had that mission, was it happening at the same time as the high energy and reckless behavior, or did that belief ever continue after your mood and energy had returned to normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure if that belief persisted after my mood and energy went back to normal. I haven't really thought about the exact timing of when those feelings actually stopped. It's just hard to remember exactly when everything transitioned back to how it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, after the high energy settled down, did you still believe for several days or longer that you had this special mission even though your sleep, activity, and impulsiveness were back to normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I don't think I still believed in the mission after my energy levels normalized. It feels like those thoughts only stuck around while I was in that high-energy state.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, about how long did the episodes usually last, and did they ever clearly last at least one full week?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods have definitely lasted much longer than just a week. In fact, I've been experiencing these episodes on and off, and sometimes continuously, for about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy episodes, did anyone say you were acting so differently that you seemed out of touch with reality, or did your belief about the mission mainly come across as grand but still understandable in the context of your mood?"

Yes, I held a very strong belief that I had a special, important mission to fulfill. To everyone around me, this made me seem completely out of touch with reality, and they thought my ideas were impossible. However, to me, it felt absolutely real and crucial at the time.
